# W11 final scored run: DeepSeek, map-reduce, fts retrieval (#250)

**Adjudicated: 18/30 (tier 1: 11/18)**, after the second read in PR #267 review round 1 (Q12 PASS -> FAIL). Blind full read (`verdicts-blind-full.json`): 19/30 (tier 1 11/18). First blind read (`verdicts-blind.json`, passages truncated by the scorer): 20/30 (tier 1 13/18). One sample per question.

Against: #243 DeepSeek baseline (pre-map-reduce) 15/30 like-for-like; W10 Claude #217 fts 24/30; #247's adjudicated eight 6/8. Same plans, hybrid-norerank on `exp/hybrid`: 22/30 adjudicated, 21/30 blind (evals/hybrid-eval/corpus-2026-11-19/deepseek/report.md).

Q12 reads FAIL in both modes under a literal `passes_if`: neither answer sets out Edward Jones's own cost structure (second reads of PR #267 and PR #268).

## How this was run

- Code: `main` 08b0ac4 (= 3326abc code), run from `tl/250-final` at 05b7282. Plan: `plans/0250-final-runs.md` (approved at d5f337d with TL conditions C1-C4).
- Snapshot `corpus-2026-11-19`, as-of 2026-11-17, Q1-Q30. Model `deepseek-v4-pro` on `https://api.deepseek.com/beta`, thinking on, request `ds-strict-1-think-on-a64000-p64000`, planner `plan-v3.1`, extract `extract-v1.1`, reduce `v5.2`, `SUBQUESTION_TOKENS=40000`, `MAP_CONCURRENCY=6`. Settings pinned in `run-scored.ps1` / `.sh`; `.env` copied in and removed, never read.
- Planner phase: 22 new calls (Q2, Q4-Q10, Q12-Q18, Q20, Q21, Q25, Q27-Q30), 8 plans reused from #246 / #247 (Q1, Q3, Q11, Q19, Q22, Q23, Q24, Q26), $0.307 new.
- Run: `--max-calls 139` (from the 0-call count: round-1 maps + one re-extraction per row key + one reduce per question), one attempt, exit 0, **79 new calls**, 67 reused. No ProviderStop, no 400, no cap reached. 67 saved records reused: 30 plans (8 inherited + 22 from the planner phase), 34 #247 map / remap records (each re-checked: rendered extract message, system prompt, schema and request byte-identical to the message it was made from, at its producing commit 7b9a5b4 / 715adda / 2d5f25b; `docs/results/w11-run/count-fts.md`), 3 reduces (Q19, Q23, Q26).
- Cost of this run: $6.59 new; $9.52 for every call the 30 answers used (input 5,106,567 tokens, 518,656 cache hits; output 2,919,410, of which reasoning 2,554,530); 124 calls off-peak, 22 peak (prices `config/pricing.yaml`, by completion time).
- Replay (0 calls, the run's `raw/` as cache): every answer identical except Q12 (see Findings).
- Scoring: blind, fresh scorers, inputs `ev250\blind-in-fts\` only (dump of the 30 answers with each cited passage, `questions.yaml`, `verdict-rule.txt`: the #217 / #243 bar, with the Q24 / Q25 / Q28 superseded behaviors). **First read** (one Sonnet scorer, sha256 `a3297da4e1584fe1...`, committed alone in 5de05e8): the scorer cut cited passages to about 110 characters and claims to 230-420 to fit the dump in its context, so some FAILs may come from truncated text. **Full read** (four Sonnet scorers, Q1-8 / Q9-15 / Q16-21 / Q22-30, each reading its lines in full; merged file `verdicts-blind-full.json`, sha256 `5bad7497a5804047150bf9b860cfeffbbd2d395aa1820f8c85a6ca014fc6e7dc`, committed alone in d8f0c60). **Second read**: the PR #267 reviewer, round 1; adjudicated = full read plus its changes.

## Changes against #243, W10 and #247

Every pipeline element changed at once between these runs and W10 (Claude, single answer call, `plan-v2`) and #243 (DeepSeek, single answer call, `plan-v2`): this run has DeepSeek `plan-v3.1` sub-questions (firm x aspect), one extraction call per firm, corrective re-search and a reduce call. So a change is labelled **pipeline + plan** (and **model** against W10) unless the scorer reasons name a narrower cause; the reasons of both reads are quoted. #247's eight: Q1, Q3, Q11, Q19, Q22, Q23, Q24, Q26 (tuned on in #247).

| Q | Change | This run (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| Q3 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | Schwab framing, priorities, named competitors, and conceded ground (long/short throttled, Advisor Network bar raised, Ameritrade attrition) sourced to 10-K/calls/deck; trade press labelled. |
| Q5 | W10 fts PASS -> FAIL | Only two claims: LeadIQ and a generic 'investments to support advisors' line; no other named platforms/initiatives with stated purpose from 51 retrieved passages, so the answer is too thin to forward. |
| Q7 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | Same dimensions across three firms with visible gaps (MS training, MS/MER headcount); borderline because MS cells are weak (2020 headcount, tangential trade-press clawback items) and Merrill column leans on BAC/GWIM and trade press. |
| Q8 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | Each firm's own language (Schwab platform/ecosystem; RJF independence/culture/affiliation choice) with RJF RCS custody vs affiliation separated in claims; borderline because the closing comparison is a thin 'difference of emphasis' and does not explicitly state the custody-vs-affiliation model difference. |
| Q10 | #243 PASS -> FAIL; W10 fts PASS -> FAIL | Expected answer but got gap_with_adjacent: declines to name or rank any firm as most explicit, so no judgment is made legible, although dated statements per firm are shown. |
| Q12 | W10 fts PASS -> FAIL | Full Schwab evidence (scale quotes, cost per account, ~40% marketing-driven, ad spend, accounts, workplace) and an explicit normative hand-off; borderline because Edward Jones's own cost structure is thin (no expense figures set alongside). |
| Q15 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | Explains cash-spread (NII $11.8B of $23.9B, client cash funding) vs asset-based fee mechanism with each model's disclosed revenue composition, and states the comparison is not same-basis; Schwab framing leans partly on trade press but 10-K supports it. |
| Q16 | #243 PASS -> FAIL; W10 fts PASS -> FAIL | Claims are organised by sub-question aspect and firm, not by position; no spread-of-positions framing, and off-topic claims (JPM data-privacy, CFPB, Schwab 24/7 trading, Fortune recognition) pad it, though dated/attributed sources and no own forecast. |
| Q17 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | Separates named deployed tools and usage results (Charley, Raimond 6,500 users, BAC 114 live GenAI cases) from plans/aspirations and risk language, and declines a false ranking; borderline as RJF is left out of the who-claims-most claim and several cited passage openings are truncated. |
| Q18 | #243 PASS -> FAIL; W10 fts PASS -> FAIL | Refused (comparison_missing) where answer was expected, although the corpus holds ADV filings, McKinsey fee-pressure research and firm fee disclosures; no distinction of disclosed changes, regulatory direction and commentary delivered. |
| Q19 | #243 FAIL -> PASS; W10 fts FAIL -> PASS | Dated, sourced cohort-preference evidence (Cerulli, BofA Private Bank 2024/2026, Schwab calls) plus EDJ's own disclosures, explicit hand-off at 'should' (C44) with honest gaps; borderline because competitor moves aimed at the cohort are thin (Schwab statements only, MS/MER/RJF not found). |
| Q21 | W10 fts PASS -> FAIL | Invalid output: no answer record (model none, reduce invalid), nothing delivered. |
| Q24 | #243 FAIL -> PASS; W10 fts FAIL -> PASS | Computes per-advisor figure only for EDJ (2,647B/20,514), declines MS/RJF/SCHW/Merrill with reasons (different populations, no headcount), and answers directly that the comparison is invalid with definition differences. |
| Q25 | #243 PASS -> FAIL; W10 fts PASS -> FAIL | Good filing-based disclosures (10-K, ADV, RJF pages) but leans on trade press for Schwab pay (recruiter quote), MS pay-threshold and clawbacks, and does not mark Schwab salary/bonus/recruiting as undisclosed; reaches for trade-press grid-type reconstruction. |
| Q26 | #243 FAIL -> PASS; W10 fts FAIL -> PASS | Corpus window holds only RJF trade press (Aug 19-Nov 12); items newest-first and in window with one why-line each, leadership gap stated; why-lines are weak filler and Clark Capital item recaps pre-window news. |
| Q28 | W10 fts PASS -> FAIL | EDJ rate (6.1% 3Q25, 6.4% 2Q25) with definition and 10-Q cite, and Merrill 'near historic lows' qualitative only, are correct; but the adjacent net headcount change and its difference from attrition is never stated plainly (only raw headcount points, Merrill's stale 2023). |

## Cost

Calls used by the 30 answers: 146 ({'plan': 30, 'map': 75, 'remap': 13, 'reduce': 28, 'answer': 0}), 79 new in this run, 67 reused. USD new $6.59, all calls $9.52, all at peak price $17.64. Mean per question: $0.317 (all calls), $0.220 (new). Per question: the Scores table. Plus the planner phase: 22 calls, $0.307.

#250 in total: 22 planner + 79 fts + 118 hybrid = 219 new calls, about $14.73.

## Findings

- Q21: the reduce call was cut at max_tokens (64,000 output tokens, `Q21-reduce-213a49aa912d57465cd7518b`): no valid answer, FAIL class i; kept, not re-called (TL ruling).
- Q20: `map.all` was cut at max_tokens (`Q20-map.all-6d65583543b5bc497c1cc684`): the question was answered without that map cell; kept, not re-called (TL ruling).
- Q12: the 0-call replay of the run (same saved map records) built a different code caveat for Q12 (series span '2024 to 2026Q3' in the run, '2024Q4 to 2026Q3' in the replay), so the replay's reduce key did not match and its answer was not reused. The scored record is the run's own answer (TL ruling). Follow-up in NEXT: a reduce caveat the code writes from saved map outputs is not deterministic.
- The first fts scorer's Q10 reason cites another question's evidence ('seen in Q9'), a cross-question reading the full read avoids.

### Q3 (answer)

- C1 In its 3Q 2026 earnings transcript (period ended September 30, 2026, published October 15, 2026), Schwab states that it has built its adviser business around helping independent advisers grow, compete, and succeed: "We have built our adviser business around helping independent advisers grow, compete and succeed." [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#10]
- C2 In its 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 25, 2026), Schwab states that management believes it can maintain its competitive position primarily through the efforts of its sales, support, technology, and business consulting teams, which are dedicated to helping RIAs grow, compete, and succeed in serving their clients: "Management believes that we can maintain our competitive position primarily through the efforts of our sales, support, technology, and business consulting teams, which are dedicated to helping RIAs grow, compete, and succeed in serving their clients." [SCHW-10K-2025#23]
- C3 Trade press (published October 28, 2026) reports that President and CEO Rick Wurster said Schwab will continue to focus on its custody services for registered investment advisors with under $100 million AUM: "Schwab will continue to focus on its custody services for registered investment advisors with under $100 million AUM, President and CEO Rick Wurster told an audience of advisors at Schwab Impact." [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-28-WM-01#0]
- C4 Trade press (published October 28, 2026) reports that Wurster said Schwab's role is to provide a platform and a support ecosystem that helps RIA firms support their clients and thrive: "Our job and our role is to provide a platform and a support ecosystem that helps you support your clients and helps your firm thrive." [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-28-WM-01#0]
- C5 In its investor day deck (published May 14, 2026), Schwab states as part of its strategy that it aims to be the leading RIA support ecosystem to help advisors serve their clients, providing wealth tools, investment and lending products, and technology in addition to custody and service: "The leading RIA support ecosystem to help advisors serve their clients, providing wealth tools, investment / lending products, & technology in addition to custody & service." [SCHW-DECK-2026-investor-day#35]
- C6 Trade press (published October 29, 2026) reports that Schwab wants to give registered investment advisors equal or better access than their wirehouse counterparts to banking services such as lending, private market investing, and long-short investment strategies: "Schwab wants to give registered investment advisors equal or better access than their wirehouse counterparts to banking services such as lending , private market investing, and long-short investment strategies." [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-29-WM-01#0]
- C7 In its 2Q 2026 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2026, published August 7, 2026), Schwab describes its Advisor Services segment as providing custodial, trading, banking and trust, and support services to independent RIAs, independent retirement advisors, and recordkeepers: "The Advisor Services segment provides custodial, trading, banking and trust, and support services to independent registered investment advisors (RIAs), independent retirement advisors, and recordkeepers." [SCHW-10Q-2026Q2#7]
- C8 In its 3Q 2026 earnings transcript (period ended September 30, 2026, published October 15, 2026), Schwab says it serves more than 16,000 RIA firms representing $5.8 trillion in assets with a platform that brings together custody, trading, banking and lending capabilities, technology, and dedicated service and support: "we serve more than 16,000 firms representing $5.8 trillion in assets with a platform that brings together custody, trading, banking and lending capabilities, technology and dedicated service and support." [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#10]
- C9 In its 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 25, 2026), Schwab says that, as custodian, it earns revenue associated with the underlying client assets, predominantly through net interest revenue and asset management and administration fees, and does not charge the RIA or end client a custody fee: "By functioning as the custodian, Schwab earns revenue associated with the underlying client assets, predominantly through net interest revenue and asset management and administration fees. In this capacity, we do not charge the RIA or end client a custody fee." [SCHW-10K-2025#26]
- C10 In its 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 25, 2026), Schwab says RIAs who custody client accounts at Schwab may use proprietary software for up-to-date client account information and trading capabilities, with the Advisor Services website as the core platform for daily business activities: "RIAs who custody client accounts at Schwab may use proprietary software that provides them with up-to-date client account information as well as trading capabilities. The Advisor Services website is the core platform for RIAs to conduct daily business activities online with Schwab." [SCHW-10K-2025#24]
- C11 In its investor day deck (published May 14, 2026), Schwab describes Advisor Services as in a position of strength and a driving force for Schwab, with $5.2 trillion in client assets, 16,000 advisory firms, 3.9 million households, and a #1 ranking in RIA custodial assets: "Advisor Services is in a position of strength & remains a driving force for Schwab. $5.2T Client Assets 16K Advisory Firms Households 3.9M in RIA Custodial Assets #1" [SCHW-DECK-2026-investor-day#62]
- C12 In its July 2026 monthly report (period ended July 31, 2026, published August 14, 2026), Schwab describes its offering to independent, fee-based investment advisors as custodial, operational, and trading support through Schwab Advisor Services: "custodial, operational and trading support for independent, fee-based investment advisors through Schwab Advisor Services TM" [SCHW-MONTHLY-2026-07#3]
- C13 In its 3Q 2025 earnings transcript (period ended September 30, 2025, published October 16, 2025), Schwab says that in its advisor services business it is deepening relationships with the 16,000 RIA firms it serves by building a broader support ecosystem beyond custody, and has grown its lending support, alternatives capability, and institutional no-transaction-fee mutual fund platform: "In our advisor services business, we're deepening relationships with the 16,000 RIA firms we serve by building a broader support ecosystem that extends beyond custody alone to add value for RIAs. We've grown our lending support, alternatives capability, and expanded our institutional no-transaction fee mutual fund platform." [SCHW-TR-2025Q3#13]
- C14 In its 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 25, 2026), Schwab says that in addition to focusing on superior service, it uses technology to provide RIAs with a highly developed, scalable platform for administering their clients' assets easily and efficiently. [SCHW-10K-2025#23]
- C15 Trade press (published October 29, 2026) reports that Wurster cited the growing use of pledged-asset lending balances, the demand for long-short investment strategies, and the need to make private-market investing more accessible to the masses as focus areas for Schwab's custody offerings: "Wurster cited the growing use of pledged-asset lending balances, the demand for long-short investment strategies, and the need to make private-market investing more accessible to the masses as focus areas for its custody offerings." [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-29-WM-01#0]
- C16 In its 4Q 2024 earnings transcript (period ended December 31, 2024, published January 21, 2025), Schwab says that in advisor services it is adding support and capabilities for RIAs of all sizes while also helping new advisors transition to independence: "In advisor services, we are adding support and capabilities for RIAs of all sizes while also helping new advisors transition to independence." [SCHW-TR-2024Q4#13]
- C17 In its 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 25, 2026), Schwab states that in the Advisor Services arena it competes with institutional custodians, wirehouses, regional and independent broker-dealers, fintech custodians, banks, and trust companies: "In the Advisor Services arena, we compete with institutional custodians, wirehouses, regional and independent broker-dealers, fintech custodians, banks, and trust companies." [SCHW-10K-2025#13]
- C18 Trade press (published October 29, 2026) reports that CEO Rick Wurster said independent advisors are competing against wirehouses that typically have strong banks attached to their advice. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-29-WM-01#0]
- C19 In its 4Q 2025 earnings transcript (period ended December 31, 2025, published January 21, 2026), CEO Rick Wurster said RIAs compete from time to time against the wealth divisions of wirehouses: "They do, from time to time, compete against the wealth divisions of wirehouses." [SCHW-TR-2025Q4#48]
- C20 In its 3Q 2026 earnings transcript (period ended September 30, 2026, published October 15, 2026), CEO Rick Wurster said Schwab changed the minimums for long/short strategies and throttled growth to some extent, while noting that advisor services NNA growth has not slowed: "as we look to October, where we have changed the minimums for long/short and throttled growth to some extent, the growth has in no way slowed at all in terms of AS NNA." [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#42]
- C21 Trade press (published October 28, 2026) reports that Wurster said Schwab limited long-short offerings to clients with at least $10 million in assets, curtailing their use by many advisors: "That led the firm to limit long-short offerings only to clients with at least $10 million in assets , curtailing their use by many advisors." [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-28-WM-01#1]
- C22 Trade press (published October 28, 2026) reports that Wurster said Schwab reduced the Schwab Advisor Network referral network by raising the bar for participation to only clients with over $5 million and RIAs with at least $500 million in AUM: "reducing the Schwab Advisor Network referral network by raising the bar for participation to only clients with over $5 million and RIAs with at least $500 million in AUM ." [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-28-WM-01#1]
- C23 In its 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 25, 2026), Schwab says that during the Ameritrade integration it experienced some expected attrition of client assets from retail accounts and RIAs, though such attrition was below its initial estimates: "we experienced some expected attrition of client assets from retail accounts and RIAs, though such attrition was below our initial estimates." [SCHW-10K-2025#141]

### Q4 (answer)

- C1 On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), CFO Alastair Borthwick said Merrill is "picking up the recruiting of experienced advisors," but that experienced recruiting "isn't an enormous part of our strategy"; the training program is what management believes it can keep driving to improve net new flows. [BAC-TR-2025Q4#106]
- C2 On the 2Q26 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2026), CFO Alastair Borthwick said the goal of management is to "just keep growing the sales force," with adviser attrition "now at near historic lows" and more to do over the next 3 to 5 years. [BAC-TR-2026Q2#63]
- C3 On the 2Q25 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2025), CFO Alastair Borthwick said Merrill and the Private Bank continued to add financial advisors through the extensive training program and experienced hiring, with approximately one-third of Q2 net new households added by newly trained advisors. [BAC-TR-2025Q2#34]
- C4 On the 1Q26 earnings call (period ended March 31, 2026), CFO Alastair Borthwick said the firm is "driving productivity higher on both newer and existing members of our financial advisers team" and continues to "attract talent across both new and experienced advisers." [BAC-TR-2026Q1#26]
- C5 Merrill's foundational beliefs in the 2025 Investor Day deck (published November 5, 2025) state that "having the best advisors enables us to deliver exceptional client outcomes." [BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#8]
- C6 This comparison can be made on this evidence and is valid for Merrill, because both items describe Merrill's strategy at different points in time. Trade press (published November 16, 2026) reports that Merrill executives said the firm largely pulled back from veteran broker recruiting at the end of 2017 but returned after Co-Presidents Lindsay Hans and Eric Schimpf took over in 2023, restoring a focus on hiring and a more aggressive offer; on the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), CFO Alastair Borthwick said Merrill is "picking up the recruiting of experienced advisors" but that experienced recruiting "isn't an enormous part of our strategy," and that the training program is what management believes it can keep driving to improve net new flows. [MER-TRADE-2026-11-16-AH-01#1, BAC-TR-2025Q4#106]
- C7 On the 2Q26 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2026), CFO Alastair Borthwick referred to the "Merrill 5% guide," a sales-force-growth target where management said it is aiming for 5% over the course of the next 3 to 5 years, and said the goal is to keep growing the sales force. [BAC-TR-2026Q2#63]
- C8 The 4Q23 supplement (period ended December 31, 2023) reports "Total wealth advisors" at period end of 18,916, with the metric defined as "Includes advisors across all wealth management businesses in GWIM and Consumer Banking"—so the figure is not Merrill alone. [BAC-SUPP-2023Q4#2]
- C9 Trade press (published November 16, 2026) reports that Merrill executives in October said recruited assets to the wealth unit doubled year-over-year as the firm returned to veteran broker recruiting. [MER-TRADE-2026-11-16-AH-01#1]
- C10 Trade press (published September 8, 2026) reports that Merrill Lynch added several advisors from rivals representing a combined $1.8 billion in client assets, including a $1.2 billion team from Morgan Stanley, a $300 million advisor from Truist, and a $285 million advisor from Wells Fargo. [MER-TRADE-2026-09-08-WM-01#0]
- C11 Trade press (published November 16, 2026) reports that a private wealth team managing $1 billion in assets at UBS Wealth Management USA jumped to Merrill Lynch in Boston, a spokesperson for the hiring firm confirmed. [MER-TRADE-2026-11-16-AH-01#0]
- C12 Merrill's Advisor Development Program recruiting page (page content as of November 3, 2026) describes the program as targeting people interested in a wealth management career with an entrepreneurial spirit, to help build a book of high-net-worth clients. [MER-PR-web-advisor-development-program#0]

### Q6 (answer)

- C1 In its 10-K for the period ended September 30, 2025 (published November 25, 2025), Raymond James states it offers multiple affiliation options, which it refers to as AdvisorChoice®: "We offer multiple affiliation options, which we refer to as AdvisorChoice®." [RJF-10K-2025#10]
- C2 In its 10-K for the period ended September 30, 2025 (published November 25, 2025), Raymond James states financial advisors primarily affiliate with it directly as employees or independent contractors, or as employees of third-party Registered Investment Advisors ("RIAs") and broker-dealers to which it provides services through its RIA and Custody Services ("RCS") division. [RJF-10K-2025#10]
- C3 In its 10-K for the period ended September 30, 2025 (published November 25, 2025), Raymond James states employee financial advisors work in a traditional branch supported by local management and administrative staff, provide services predominantly to retail clients, and receive compensation that primarily includes a payout on revenues they generate plus participation in the firm's employee benefit plans. [RJF-10K-2025#10]
- C4 In its 10-K for the period ended September 30, 2025 (published November 25, 2025), Raymond James states its independent contractor financial advisors are generally responsible for all of their direct costs and, accordingly, receive a higher payout percentage on the revenues they generate than employee financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#11]
- C5 In its 10-K for the period ended September 30, 2025 (published November 25, 2025), Raymond James states independent contractor financial advisors may affiliate with it directly or through an affiliated bank or credit union in its Financial Institutions Division. [RJF-10K-2025#11]
- C6 In its 10-K for the period ended September 30, 2025 (published November 25, 2025), Raymond James states that through its domestic RCS division it offers third-party RIAs and broker-dealers a range of products and services including custodial services, trade execution, research, and other support, for which it receives fees that may be either transactional or based on AUA. [RJF-10K-2025#12]
- C7 In its 10-K for the period ended September 30, 2025 (published November 25, 2025), Raymond James states firms affiliated with it through RCS retain the fees they charge to their clients and are generally responsible for all of their direct costs, and that financial advisors associated with firms in RCS are not included in its financial advisor counts although their client assets are included in AUA. [RJF-10K-2025#12]
- C8 On an undated web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James describes AdvisorChoice® as 'The Power of Choice,' where advisors can match their professional goals to the affiliation option that is right for them, each offering a unique combination of support, freedom and flexibility, and all backed by the full support of a leading independent financial firm. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#0]
- C9 Raymond James' investor day deck (published June 5, 2025) states the movement to 'independence' is not channel-specific. [RJF-DECK-2025-investor-day#38]
- C10 On the earnings call for the period ended December 31, 2025 (published January 28, 2026), Raymond James' CEO said the firm has the largest addressable market across its affiliation options—from employee to independent contractor to RIA custody—and has critical mass and decades of experience in all of them. [RJF-TR-2025Q4#50]
- C11 On the earnings call for the period ended June 30, 2026 (published July 22, 2026), Raymond James' CEO said the firm has the largest addressable market in the industry because it has all the affiliation options an advisor could possibly want, and the firm is agnostic about how an advisor affiliates. [RJF-TR-2026Q2#26]
- C12 On an undated web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James lists the Traditional Employee affiliation model payout at up to 50% plus a full array of competitive employee benefits offerings. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C13 On an undated web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James lists the Advisor Select affiliation model payout at 70-80% gross payout plus a full array of competitive employee benefits offerings. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C14 On an undated web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James lists the Independent Contractor affiliation model payout at 81% to 90%, leveraging third-party partnerships to create a benefits package. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C15 On an undated web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James lists the Bank & Credit Union affiliation model as requiring a minimum 3 years of experience and $250,000 in production. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#2]
- C16 On an undated web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James lists the Corporate RIA affiliation model as requiring a minimum 3 years of experience and $50 million in assets under advisement. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#2]
- C17 On an undated web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James lists the RIA & Custody Services model as requiring $100 million or more in assets to establish a new RIA (the requirement for SEC registration) or a minimum of $30 million to plug in to an existing RIA. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#2]
- C18 On an undated web page (page content as of March 11, 2026), Raymond James describes Advisor Select® as an employee affiliation option providing established advisors the full benefits of the employee channel with the independence to create their own branches. [RJF-PR-web-advisor-select#0]
- C19 On an undated web page (page content as of July 12, 2026), Raymond James describes its RIA & Custody Services division as giving independent RIAs true strategic, financial and administrative control to grow their business their way, using as much or as little of the firm's support as needed. [RJF-PR-web-independent-ria-custody-services#0]
- C20 Whether the mix across the affiliation models has shifted over the periods disclosed: on this evidence the comparison is valid only in part. The 10-K discloses channel headcounts rather than a mix series—as of September 30, 2023, 2024 and 2025, employee financial advisors were 3,693, 3,826 and 3,878, and independent contractor financial advisors were 5,019, 4,961 and 5,065, with financial advisors associated with RCS firms excluded from those counts—so these are headcounts by channel, not a computed mix. Management's own statements do describe a shift: on the fiscal fourth quarter 2024 earnings call, the then-CEO said that in fiscal 2024 the employee channel led recruiting by total dollars after the independent channel had led a couple of years earlier, and that RCS had the highest percentage growth; on the fiscal first quarter 2026 earnings call, the CEO said recruiting activity is broad-based across affiliation options but "maybe more heavily tilted in the last six months on the independent contractor side of the business"; and on the fiscal second quarter 2026 earnings call, the CEO said much more of its recruiting over the last year has come from the independent side versus the employee side, so "there's just a mix shift there to some extent." [RJF-10K-2025#227, RJF-10K-2025#12, RJF-TR-2024Q3#66, RJF-TR-2025Q4#25, RJF-TR-2026Q1#44]
- A1 As of September 30, 2025, Raymond James had 3,878 employee financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227]
- A2 As of September 30, 2025, Raymond James had 5,065 independent contractor financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227]
- A3 As of September 30, 2024, Raymond James had 3,826 employee financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227]
- A4 As of September 30, 2024, Raymond James had 4,961 independent contractor financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227]
- A5 As of September 30, 2023, Raymond James had 3,693 employee financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227]
- A6 As of September 30, 2023, Raymond James had 5,019 independent contractor financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227]
- A7 Raymond James states financial advisors associated with firms in its RCS division are not included in its financial advisor counts, although their client assets are included in AUA. [RJF-10K-2025#12]

- C1 The Charles Schwab Corporation: In its 3Q26 earnings transcript (period ended September 30, 2026), Schwab states that it has built its advisor business around helping independent advisors grow, compete and succeed: "We have built our adviser business around helping independent advisers grow, compete and succeed." [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#10]

- C5 The Charles Schwab Corporation: In its 2025 Form 10-K (Advisor Services segment, period ended December 31, 2025), Schwab states that its sales, support, technology, and business consulting teams are "dedicated to helping RIAs grow, compete, and succeed in serving their clients." [SCHW-10K-2025#23]
- C6 The Charles Schwab Corporation: In its 2025 Form 10-K (Advisor Services segment, period ended December 31, 2025), Schwab states that it utilizes technology to provide RIAs with a highly-developed, scalable platform for administering their clients' assets easily and efficiently. [SCHW-10K-2025#23]
- C7 The Charles Schwab Corporation: In its 2025 Form 10-K (Advisor Services segment, period ended December 31, 2025), Schwab states that "Advisor Services sponsors and hosts a variety of national, regional, local, and virtual events designed to help RIAs of all sizes and complexities identify and implement better ways to expand and efficiently manage their practices." - C8 The Charles Schwab Corporation: In its 2026 investor day deck (published May 14, 2026), Schwab describes itself as "the leading RIA support ecosystem to help advisors serve their clients, providing wealth tools, investment / lending products, & technology in addition to custody & service." [SCHW-DECK-2026-investor-day#35]
- C9 The Charles Schwab Corporation: In its 3Q25 earnings transcript (period ended September 30, 2025), Schwab states that "we're deepening relationships with the 16,000 RIA firms we serve by building a broader support ecosystem that extends beyond custody alone to add value for RIAs." [SCHW-TR-2025Q3#13]
- C10 The Charles Schwab Corporation: In its 3Q26 investor deck (period ended September 30, 2026, published October 15, 2026), Schwab describes its differentiated, full-service platform for advisors as comprising scale and reputation, breadth beyond custody, operational excellence, and dedicated service and support. [SCHW-DECK-2026Q3#21]
- C11 The Charles Schwab Corporation: In its 3Q26 earnings transcript (period ended September 30, 2026), Schwab states that "our capabilities are hard to match in terms of the service we provide, the breadth of capabilities that we provide, our banking and lending capabilities that we provide alongside our custody, the AI innovations that we're making on behalf of clients, the advice and consulting support that we provide to clients." [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#52]

- C13 The Charles Schwab Corporation: Trade press reports (published October 28, 2026) that Schwab's CEO states its job and role is to provide a platform and a support ecosystem that helps advisors support their clients and helps their firms thrive—"Our job and our role is to provide a platform and a support ecosystem that helps you support your clients and helps your firm thrive"—rather than telling them what they should become. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-28-WM-01#0]
- C14 The Charles Schwab Corporation: Trade press reports (published October 29, 2026) that Schwab's CEO states the firm is increasingly trying to play the role of being advisors' bank and helping them manage both sides of the balance sheet for their clients: "We are increasingly trying to play a role of being their bank and helping them manage both sides of the balance sheet for their clients." [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-29-WM-01#0]

[RJF-PR-web-independent-advisor#0]
- C17 Raymond James Financial: On its independent advisor page (as of May 19, 2026), Raymond James says independent advisors can grow their own practice with 'powerful independence,' with everything the firm has available when the advisor wants it, supported by a full-service, self-clearing NYSE-member broker/dealer. [RJF-PR-web-independent-advisor#0]

[RJF-TR-2025Q2#7]
- C22 Raymond James Financial: In its earnings transcript for the period ended September 30, 2025 (published October 22, 2025), CEO Paul Shoukry described the best-of-both-worlds value proposition as an advisor- and client-focused culture coupled with leading technology and solutions, adding that the strong balance sheet and commitment to independence is proving a differentiator for advisors evaluating alternatives: "Our best-of-both-worlds value proposition, where we offer a unique combination of an advisor and client-focused culture coupled with leading technology and solutions, continues to resonate with advisors across all of our affiliation options." [RJF-TR-2025Q3#8]
- C23 Raymond James Financial: In its earnings transcript for the period ended December 31, 2025 (published January 28, 2026), CEO Paul Shoukry said Raymond James offers a unique combination of an advisor- and client-focused culture coupled with leading technology and solutions, and that this value proposition, with the strong balance sheet and commitment to independence, is a differentiator for advisors evaluating alternatives: "We offer a unique combination of an advisor and client-focused culture, coupled with leading technology and solutions. This value proposition, coupled with our strong balance sheet and commitment to independence, is proving to be a differentiator for advisors evaluating alternatives." [RJF-TR-2025Q4#5]
- C24 Raymond James Financial: In its earnings transcript for the period ended March 31, 2026 (published April 22, 2026), CEO Paul Shoukry said at Raymond James advisers do not have to choose between culture and capabilities, citing a unique combination of an adviser- and client-focused culture with leading technologies, products and solutions, a strong balance sheet, long-term thinking, and commitment to independence: "At Raymond James, advisers do not have to choose between culture and capabilities. We offer a unique combination of an adviser and client-focused culture together with leading technologies, products and solutions advisers need to serve clients at a high level." [RJF-TR-2026Q1#5]
- C25 Raymond James Financial: In its earnings transcript for the period ended June 30, 2026 (published July 22, 2026), CEO Paul Shoukry said the combination of culture and capabilities, with the firm's strong balance sheet, long-term focus and commitment to independence, continues to set Raymond James apart for advisers evaluating alternatives: "Together with our strong balance sheet, long-term focus and commitment to independence, that combination continues to set Raymond James apart for advisers evaluating alternatives." [RJF-TR-2026Q2#7]
- C26 Raymond James Financial: In its earnings transcript for the period ended December 31, 2025 (published January 28, 2026), CEO Paul Shoukry said Raymond James has the largest addressable market across its affiliation options, from employee to independent contractor to RIA custody, with critical mass and decades of experience in each, and that the value proposition of cultural fit, platform, and multiple affiliation options appeals to advisors from many firms: "we have the largest addressable market across our affiliation options, from employee to independent contractor to the RIA custody, and we have critical mass and decades of experience in all of them." [RJF-TR-2025Q4#50]
- C27 Raymond James Financial: On its affiliation options page (as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James describes AdvisorChoice as letting advisors match their professional goals to the right affiliation option, each offering a unique combination of support, freedom and flexibility, all backed by a leading independent financial firm. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#0]
- C28 Raymond James Financial: In its 2025 Form 10-K (Private Client Group, period ended September 30, 2025, published November 25, 2025), Raymond James states that its independent contractor financial advisor options are designed to help advisors build their businesses with as much or as little of the firm's support as they determine they need: "Our independent contractor financial advisor options are designed to help our advisors build their businesses with as much or as little of our support as they determine they need." [RJF-10K-2025#11]
- C29 Raymond James Financial: An advisor testimonial in Raymond James's 2025 investor day deck (published June 5, 2025) describes the firm's long-standing culture of promoting independence and putting advisors and their clients first, plus access to world-class technology. [RJF-DECK-2025-investor-day#110]
- C30 The Charles Schwab Corporation and Raymond James Financial: Neither firm makes a direct statement comparing its own value proposition with the other's, but the comparison can still be made on this evidence because each firm describes its value proposition to independent advisors in its own words. Schwab frames its value proposition around a full-service platform and support ecosystem—custody, trading, banking and lending, technology, and dedicated service and support—built to help independent advisors grow, compete and succeed. Raymond James frames its value proposition around independence paired with the firm's support: partnership in independence, freedom and control combined with robust support, and an advisor- and client-focused culture coupled with leading technology and solutions, a strong balance sheet and commitment to independence. The difference is one of emphasis rather than a directly opposed claim: Schwab emphasizes the breadth of the platform and ecosystem it provides, while Raymond James emphasizes independence, culture, and choice across affiliation options. [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#10, SCHW-10K-2025#23, SCHW-DECK-2026-investor-day#35, RJF-PR-web-independent-advisor#0, RJF-PR-web-independent-ria-custody-services#0, RJF-TR-2025Q2#7, RJF-TR-2025Q4#5]

### Q9 (answer)

- C1 Morgan Stanley: The 1Q26 10-Q (period ended March 31, 2026) describes its Wealth Management segment as providing 'a comprehensive array of financial services and solutions to individual investors, including high and ultra-high net worth individuals, and businesses and institutions,' and as supporting clients 'through three channels: Advisor-Led, Self-Directed and Workplace.' [MS-10Q-2026Q1#16]

- C3 Merrill: Bank of America's investor-day deck (published November 5, 2025) states that Merrill and the Private Bank are 'Serving the wealth continuum of clients.' [BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#9]
- C4 Merrill: Bank of America's investor-day deck (published November 5, 2025) states that it believes 'the Wealth Continuum allows us to deliver as one company and is our engine for lasting growth.' [BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#8]
- C5 Merrill: Bank of America's 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that 'Merrill Wealth Management’s advisory business provides a high-touch client experience through a network of financial advisors focused on clients with over $250,000 in total investable assets.' [BAC-10K-2025#246]
- C6 Morgan Stanley: The 2Q26 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2026) labels its wealth management clients by wealth as 'high net worth' and 'ultra-high net worth' individuals, in describing Wealth Management as serving individual investors 'including high and ultra-high net worth individuals.' [MS-10Q-2026Q2#16]
- C7 Morgan Stanley: The 2Q26 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2026) states that Wealth Management 'supports clients through three channels: Advisor-Led, Self-Directed and Workplace.' [MS-10Q-2026Q2#16]
- C8 Morgan Stanley: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) refers to its loan customers as including 'high to ultra-high net worth individuals': 'We provide loans and lending commitments to a variety of customers, including large corporate and institutional clients, as well as high to ultra-high net worth individuals.' [MS-10K-2025#321]
- C9 Merrill: Bank of America's 2Q26 deck (period ended June 30, 2026) uses the labels 'Merrill primary households' and 'Private Bank core relationships' for its digitally active household/relationship metrics, defining them as 'Merrill primary households ($250K+ in investable assets within the enterprise)' and 'Private Bank core relationships ($3MM+ in total balances).' [BAC-DECK-2026Q2#20]
- C10 Merrill: Bank of America's investor-day deck (published November 5, 2025) defines the Mass Affluent tier as '$500K - $1MM' ('Mass Affluent ($500K - $1MM)'). [BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#12]
- C11 Merrill: Bank of America's investor-day deck (published November 5, 2025) defines the High Net Worth tier as '$1MM - $10MM' ('High Net Worth ($1MM - $10MM)'). [BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#12]
- C12 Merrill: Merrill's investor-day deck (published November 5, 2025) reports a '$500K+' household segment, with '750K $500K+ households.' [BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#18]
- C13 Merrill: On the 3Q26 earnings call (October 2026), Bank of America said that 'Merrill added about 4,200 net new households with at least $500,000 in assets, up 17%.' [BAC-TR-2026Q3#19]
- C14 Merrill: On the 3Q26 earnings call (October 2026), Bank of America said that 'the Private Bank added 470 net new relationships with at least $3 million under management.' [BAC-TR-2026Q3#19]
- C15 Morgan Stanley: The 3Q25 10-Q (period ended September 30, 2025) states: 'We provide loans and lending commitments to a variety of customers, including large corporate and institutional clients, as well as high to ultra-high net worth individuals.' [MS-10Q-2025Q3#154]
- C16 Morgan Stanley: The 2Q26 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2026) states that 'Other loans primarily include certain loans originated in the tailored lending business within the Wealth Management business segment, which typically consist of bespoke lending arrangements provided to ultra-high net worth clients. These facilities are generally secured by eligible collateral.' [MS-10Q-2026Q2#381]
- C17 Morgan Stanley: On the 3Q26 earnings call (October 2026), management referenced 'ultra-high net worth family office infrastructure solutions.' [MS-TR-2026Q3#33]
- C18 Morgan Stanley: The 2Q26 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2026) states that such CRE loans 'are originated through our private banking platform, are both secured and generally benefiting from full or partial guarantees from high or ultra-high net worth clients.' [MS-10Q-2026Q2#186]
- C19 Merrill: Bank of America's 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that 'Merrill Wealth Management provides tailored solutions to meet clients’ needs through a full set of investment management, brokerage, banking and retirement products.' [BAC-10K-2025#246]
- C20 Merrill: Bank of America's 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that 'Bank of America Private Bank, together with Merrill Wealth Management’s Private Wealth Management business, provides comprehensive wealth management solutions targeted to high net worth and ultra high net worth clients, as well as customized solutions to meet clients’ wealth structuring, investment management, trust and banking needs, including specialty asset management services.' [BAC-10K-2025#246]
- C21 Merrill: Merrill's recruiting page (page content as of November 3, 2026) states that Merrill offers 'robust solutions for ultra-high-net-worth clients, including alternative investments and sustainable and impact investing strategies.' [MER-PR-web-becoming-a-merrill-lynch-advisor#0]
- C22 Merrill: Trade press reports that a team joining Merrill Private Wealth Management 'specializes in serving high-net-worth and ultra-high-net-worth clients and will be based in Merrill’s Northern Virginia market and aligned with Merrill’s Southeast private wealth division.' [MER-TRADE-2026-09-08-WM-01#0]
- C23 Morgan Stanley and Merrill: The two firms' segmentation approaches can be compared on this evidence, but their wealth thresholds are not comparable like-for-like. Morgan Stanley's 2Q26 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2026) describes Wealth Management as providing 'a comprehensive array of financial services and solutions to individual investors, including high and ultra-high net worth individuals, and businesses and institutions' and supporting clients 'through three channels: Advisor-Led, Self-Directed and Workplace'; the searched documents state no dollar thresholds for those high/ultra-high net worth tiers. Merrill instead publishes a named 'Wealth Continuum' with dollar-banded tiers — 'Mass Affluent ($500K - $1MM)' and 'High Net Worth ($1MM - $10MM)' (Bank of America investor-day deck, published November 5, 2025) — plus additional dollar thresholds such as 'Merrill primary households ($250K+ in investable assets within the enterprise)' and 'Private Bank core relationships ($3MM+ in total balances)' (Bank of America 2Q26 deck, period ended June 30, 2026). The difference is one of approach: Morgan Stanley labels clients by broad wealth category and channel and does not publish tier thresholds in the searched documents, while Merrill segments clients along a wealth continuum defined by explicit dollar ranges and thresholds. [MS-10Q-2026Q2#232, MS-SUPP-2026Q3#38, BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#12, BAC-DECK-2026Q2#20]
- A1 Morgan Stanley: The 3Q26 supplement (period ended September 30, 2026) states that 'Self-directed client assets represent active accounts which are not advisor-led. Active accounts are defined as having at least $25 in assets'; the passages do not state wealth thresholds or minimum investable assets for the high or ultra-high net worth tiers. [MS-SUPP-2026Q3#38]
- G Morgan Stanley - The wealth thresholds or minimum investable assets that define each tier or client segment: not published in the documents searched; the closest disclosed figure is Morgan Stanley defines 'active accounts' in the Self-Directed channel as having at least $25 in assets; the passages do not state wealth thresholds or minimum (adjacent).

### Q10 (gap_with_adjacent)

- C1 On this evidence, the comparison of which firm has been most explicit about serving mass-affluent clients is not valid across the five firms: there is no shared 'mass-affluent' definition to rank them by, because the firms describe different client segments. Bank of America's GWIM investor-day deck (published November 5, 2025) is the only cited material that explicitly names a 'Mass Affluent ($500K - $1MM)' segment within its 'Wealth Continuum' and states that Merrill and the Private Bank are 'Serving the wealth continuum of clients.' Morgan Stanley's cited Wealth Management description names 'high and ultra-high net worth individuals' rather than mass-affluent clients; Schwab's cited statements describe 'affluent' and 'higher net-worth' clients rather than mass-affluent clients; and the cited Edward Jones and Raymond James materials contain no mass-affluent statement. Because these are different client-segment definitions, the five firms cannot be ranked against one another for being 'most explicit about serving mass-affluent clients.' [BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#12, BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#9, MS-10Q-2026Q1#16, SCHW-TR-2025Q4#11, SCHW-TR-2024Q4#11]

### Q13 (answer)

- C1 The 2Q26 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2026) states that the firm is working on a referral model for workplace participants who do not yet have an advice-based relationship, which can begin as early as financial education and includes 401(k) plans and new 401(k) assets, to help them understand where they could use advice. Management said: "for those that might not yet have the advice-based relationship, we're working on that referral model. And that's something that can begin as early as the financial education spot, and it can also begin -- like I said, we have examples with 401(k)s and new 401(k) assets that come in that is not necessarily an IPO-specific workplace where we're really giving people that ability to better understand where they could use advice." [MS-TR-2026Q2#28]
- C2 On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), the firm said Morgan Stanley at Work, through its exclusive partnership with Carta, positions Morgan Stanley as an early trusted advisor to more than 50,000 private companies, and that employees across its workplace companies benefit from management of equity compensation plans, liquidity opportunities, and full-service advice. [MS-TR-2025Q4#12]
- C3 On the 4Q24 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2024), the firm said its exclusive partnership with Carta means Carta will refer private companies with stock plans to Morgan Stanley as they move toward going public, and that the firm is committed to making that transition a seamless experience. [MS-TR-2024Q4#77]
- C4 On the 3Q25 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2025), the firm said the Carta relationship began as a referral-based model under which companies moving from the private side to the public side were referred to Morgan Stanley, and that it has seen conversions into its public-side business during the year. [MS-TR-2025Q3#42]
- C5 On the 3Q26 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2026), the firm said clients are brought to E*TRADE through the corporate stock plan (workplace) context and, where appropriate, become fee-based flows to advisers: "we are very excited about the attachment points ultimately that bring clients to E*TRADE, that we are close with them in the corporate stock plan context, i.e., workplace, but then ultimately, where it's appropriate, fee-based flows that make their way to the advisers." [MS-TR-2026Q3#23]
- C6 On the 3Q25 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2025), the firm said it uses LeadIQ, an AI-powered lead distribution platform, to match workplace and self-directed relationships and facilitate engagement with financial advisors: "LeadIQ, our AI-powered lead distribution platform, focusing on matching workplace and self-directed relationships and facilitating engagement with our financial advisors." [MS-TR-2025Q3#8]
- C7 On the 2Q26 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2026), the firm said its investments go into referral models and products like LeadIQ to match individuals with advisers, with the goal of being their principal financial adviser: "They're going into creating our referral models. They're going into products like LeadIQ that will allow us to match individuals with advisers. And our goal is to be their principal financial adviser." [MS-TR-2026Q2#23]
- C8 On the 2Q26 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2026), the firm said it offers financial advice and financial wellness to clients, uses technology to match advisers to the right clients, and offers products, alternatives, and different types of solutions across different points in client life cycles. [MS-TR-2026Q2#60]
- C9 On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), the firm said it is using its scale to invest in broadening financial-advisor capabilities, including alternatives and privates, tax-efficient investing, digitized assets, family office and OCIO, and tailored lending: "We are using our scale to invest in broadening capabilities for FAs that are difficult for others to replicate: alternatives and privates, tax-efficient investing, digitized assets, family office and OCIO, and tailored lending." [MS-TR-2025Q4#9]
- C10 On the 2Q26 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2026), management said the firm has about 70% of the top 100 unicorns by market cap in its workplace pipeline and has invested to service these companies from early stages: "we have about 70% of the top 100 unicorns by market cap in terms of our workplace pipeline. So we've spent a lot of time thinking about how do we service some of these companies at the very early stages." [MS-TR-2026Q2#21]
- C11 On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), management described the financial advisor, Workplace, and E*TRADE channels as each a category leader that together comprise the firm's strategic client acquisition model: "Our financial advisors, Workplace, and E*TRADE, each a category leader, which taken together comprise our strategic client acquisition model." [MS-TR-2025Q4#7]
- C12 On the 4Q24 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2024), management said investments in self-directed and Workplace channels drive the firm's differentiated client acquisition funnel: "In Wealth Management, investments in our self-directed and Workplace channels drive our differentiated client acquisition funnel." [MS-TR-2024Q4#9]
- C13 On the 3Q25 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2025), management described the workplace channel as a powerful asset acquisition tool: "further evidence that our workplace channel serves as a powerful asset acquisition tool." [MS-TR-2025Q3#15]
- C14 On the 2Q26 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2026), management said Workplace brings relationships and assets onto the platform and that the firm is well positioned to support these new relationships: "Workplace brings relationships and assets onto the platform, and we are well positioned to support these new relationships." [MS-TR-2026Q2#16]
- C15 On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), the firm reported that advisor-led assets originating from workplace and E*TRADE relationships accelerated to a record $99 billion for full-year 2025, compared with historical averages of around $60 billion per year: "Advisor-led assets originating from workplace and E*TRADE relationships accelerated, growing to a record $99 billion for the full year, compared to historical averages of around $60 billion per year." [MS-TR-2025Q4#20]
- C16 On the 1Q26 earnings call (period ended March 31, 2026), the firm reported that since 2020 it has generated over $400 billion of new adviser-led assets from relationships that originated from either Workplace or E*TRADE: "Since 2020, we have generated over $400 billion of new adviser-led assets from relationships that originated from either Workplace or E*TRADE." [MS-TR-2026Q1#13]
- C17 On the 1Q26 earnings call (period ended March 31, 2026), the firm said that, inclusive of Workplace assets on its platform prior to the acquisition of E*TRADE, the total value of adviser-led assets sourced from Workplace and E*TRADE exceeds $1.2 trillion: "inclusive of Workplace assets on our platform prior to the acquisition of E*TRADE, the total value of adviser-led assets sourced from Workplace and E*TRADE exceeds $1.2 trillion." [MS-TR-2026Q1#13]
- C18 On the 1Q26 earnings call (period ended March 31, 2026), the firm said this represents roughly 20% of its current $5.8 trillion of adviser-led assets: "This represents roughly 20% of our current $5.8 trillion of adviser-led assets." [MS-TR-2026Q1#13]
- C19 On the 1Q25 earnings call (period ended March 31, 2025), the firm reported that assets migrating to the advisor-led channel that originated from the workplace channel grew by an incremental $20 billion in the quarter, adding to roughly $300 billion accumulated from workplace since 2020: "assets migrating to the advisor-led channel that originated from the workplace channel as these relationships grow, with an incremental $20 billion this quarter, adding to the roughly $300 billion accumulated from workplace since we expanded our channel in 2020." [MS-TR-2025Q1#13]
- C20 On the 2Q25 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2025), the firm said 70% of the flows that originate from workplace are net new assets to the firm: "70% of the flows that come from workplace, that originate from workplace, is actually net new assets to the firm." [MS-TR-2025Q2#34]
- C21 On the 3Q25 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2025), the firm said it has previously cited about $60 billion of workplace migration per year and, three quarters into 2025, was exceeding those numbers on a full-year basis: "we've said that we've seen about $60 billion of migration per year. We're already three quarters into it, and we're exceeding those numbers from a full-year basis." [MS-TR-2025Q3#25]
- C22 On the 2Q26 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2026), the firm said stock plan IPO flows represented just over half of overall net new assets in the quarter: "Stock plan IPO flows represented just over half of the overall NNA this quarter." [MS-TR-2026Q2#16]
- C23 On the 3Q26 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2026), the firm said IPO-related flows once again contributed just over half of overall net new assets: "IPO-related flows once again contributed to just over half of the overall NNA." [MS-TR-2026Q3#15]
- C24 On the 1Q26 earnings call (period ended March 31, 2026), the firm said Workplace stood out as having sourced clients who continue to aggregate assets onto the platform and benefit from stock plan vesting events: "Workplace stood out as having sourced clients who continue to aggregate assets onto our platform and benefit from stock plan vesting events." [MS-TR-2026Q1#11]
- C25 On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), the firm said Workplace and E*TRADE relationships continue to seek out advice for full-year 2025: "Workplace and E-Trade relationships continue to seek out advice." [MS-TR-2025Q4#19]
- C26 On the 1Q26 earnings call (period ended March 31, 2026), the firm said it saw greater retention of vested Workplace assets in the quarter, which translated into net new assets, and that channel migration has helped contribute to over $1 trillion of total assets in its adviser-led strategy. [MS-TR-2026Q1#24]

### Q14 (answer)

- C1 Raymond James Financial: On its affiliation-options web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James states that through AdvisorChoice financial advisors can choose among six affiliation options—Traditional Employee, Advisor Select, Independent Contractor, Bank & Credit Union, Corporate RIA, and RIA & Custody Services—each offering a unique combination of support, freedom and flexibility backed by the full support of a leading independent financial firm: “Through AdvisorChoice®, you have the ability to match your professional goals to the affiliation option that’s right for you, each offering a unique combination of support, freedom and flexibility – and all backed by the full support of a leading independent financial firm.” [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#0]
- C2 Raymond James Financial: The investor day deck published June 5, 2025 shows the AdvisorChoice spectrum spanning Employee Advisor (RJA), Advisor Select, Independent Advisor (ICD), Banks and Credit Unions, IAR (Corporate RIA), and RIA & Custody Services: “EMPLOYEE ADVISOR (RJA) ADVISOR SELECT INDEPENDENT ADVISOR (ICD) BANKS AND CREDIT UNIONS IAR* (CORPORATE RIA) RIA** & CUSTODY SERVICES”. [RJF-DECK-2025-investor-day#38]
- C3 Raymond James Financial: In its 2025 Form 10-K (period ended September 30, 2025), Raymond James states that financial advisors primarily affiliate with it directly as either employees or independent contractors, or as employees of third-party RIAs and broker-dealers to which it provides services through its RIA and Custody Services (RCS) division: “Financial advisors primarily affiliate with us directly as either employees or independent contractors or as employees of third-party Registered Investment Advisors (“RIAs”) and broker-dealers to which we provide services through our RIA and Custody Services (“RCS”) division.” [RJF-10K-2025#10]
- C4 Raymond James Financial: On its affiliation-options web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James states that the Traditional Employee affiliation option offers up to 50% payout and a full array of competitive employee benefits offerings: “Up to 50% payout** and full array of competitive employee benefits offerings.” [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C5 Raymond James Financial: On its affiliation-options web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James states that the Advisor Select affiliation option offers a 70-80% gross payout and a full array of competitive employee benefits offerings: “70-80% gross payout** and full array of competitive employee benefits offerings.” [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C6 Raymond James Financial: On its affiliation-options web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James states that the Independent Contractor affiliation option offers an 81% to 90% payout, with benefits built through third-party partnerships: “81% to 90% payout***. Leverage our third party partnerships to create a benefits package that best fits your team.” [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C7 Raymond James Financial: On its affiliation-options web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James states that the Corporate RIA affiliation option offers an 81% to 90% payout, with benefits built through third-party partnerships: “81% to 90% payout***. Leverage our third-party partnerships to create a benefits package that best fits your team.” [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#2]
- C8 Raymond James Financial: On its affiliation-options web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James states that the Bank & Credit Union affiliation option provides a competitive compensation package from the financial institution in partnership with Raymond James during the advisor's transition: “Get the support you need during your transition by receiving a competitive compensation package from the financial institution in partnership with Raymond James.” [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#2]
- C9 Raymond James Financial: On its affiliation-options web page (page content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James states that under the RIA & Custody Services model, advisors work with their RIA or broker/dealer to determine compensation and create their own benefits package: “Work with your RIA or broker/dealer to determine compensation. Create your own benefits package.” [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#2]
- C10 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended March 31, 2026 (published April 22, 2026), Raymond James explained that independent payouts are higher because independent advisors cover their overhead costs, real estate and health insurance, and that the employee side has tiered payout systems where advisors move higher up the grid as production increases; it added that much more of its recruiting over the last year has come from the independent side versus the employee side: “the independent payouts are higher because they cover their overhead costs, their real estate, their health insurance, et cetera, as you know, and so over the last year, in particular, much more of our recruiting has come from the independent side of the business versus the employee side of the business.” [RJF-TR-2026Q1#44]
- C11 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended June 30, 2026 (published July 22, 2026), Raymond James stated that some prospective advisors do not know which affiliation option they want and that the firm works with them to evaluate the right affiliation option and its pros and cons: “they’re actually interested in understanding the various options and the various affiliation options, which those are the best conversations to have, which like we don’t necessarily know exactly how we want to affiliate with Raymond James and how we want to run our business going forward.” [RJF-TR-2026Q2#31]
- C12 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended June 30, 2026 (published July 22, 2026), management said the firm has the largest addressable market in the industry because it has all the affiliation options an advisor could want, and that it is agnostic about how an advisor affiliates as long as the advisor embraces the firm's culture, values and platform: “we have the largest addressable market in the industry because we have all the affiliation options that an adviser could possibly want. And we have adviser choice where we’re agnostic into how you affiliate with Raymond James.” [RJF-TR-2026Q2#26]
- C13 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended December 31, 2025 (published January 28, 2026), management said the firm has the largest addressable market across its affiliation options, from employee to independent contractor to RIA custody, with critical mass and decades of experience in all of them, and that it is not a new venture: “we have the largest addressable market across our affiliation options, from employee to independent contractor to the RIA custody, and we have critical mass and decades of experience in all of them. It’s not a new venture for us.” [RJF-TR-2025Q4#50]
- C14 Raymond James Financial: In the investor day deck published June 5, 2025, management describes the model as combining multiple affiliation options, the best of both worlds, advisor and book ownership, and RJF stability to inspire and empower the world's best financial professionals. [RJF-DECK-2025-investor-day#49]
- C15 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended June 30, 2026 (published July 22, 2026), management said advisors do not have to choose between culture and capabilities because the firm offers an advisor- and client-focused culture together with leading technology, products and solutions: “advisers do not have to choose between culture and capabilities. We offer a unique combination of an adviser and client-focused culture along with the leading technology, products and solutions advisers need to serve clients at a high level.” [RJF-TR-2026Q2#6]
- C16 Raymond James Financial: In the investor day deck published June 5, 2025, management lists the Private Client Group's strategic initiatives for the affiliation model as retention, advisor productivity and recruiting: “RETENTION ADVISOR PRODUCTIVITY RECRUIT ING”. [RJF-DECK-2025-investor-day#40]
- C17 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended June 30, 2026 (published July 22, 2026), management stated that growth starts with keeping existing advisors satisfied and having high retention of existing advisors: “it starts with keeping our existing advisers satisfied and having high retention of our existing advisers.” [RJF-TR-2026Q2#27]
- C18 Raymond James Financial: In its 2025 Form 10-K (period ended September 30, 2025), the firm states its culture is rooted in putting clients first, acting with integrity, thinking long term, and valuing independence: “we put clients first, • we act with integrity, • we think long term, and • we value independence.” [RJF-10K-2025#24]
- C19 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended December 31, 2025 (published January 28, 2026), Raymond James said advisors are coming to it from wires, regionals and other independents, rather than from one firm: “Advisors are coming from, you know, wires, regionals, other independents.” [RJF-TR-2025Q4#50]
- C20 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended June 30, 2025 (published July 23, 2025), Raymond James described private-equity-backed roll-ups as a relatively novel and aggressive competitive dynamic in advisor recruiting: “The dynamic that’s relatively novel in the last five years is these PE-backed roll-ups cropping up everywhere.” [RJF-TR-2025Q2#44]
- C21 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended September 30, 2025 (published October 22, 2025), Raymond James contrasted itself with competitors that compete with the largest check and change their compensation plans each year to cross-sell more bank products, saying: “many of our competitors compete with the largest check.” [RJF-TR-2025Q3#26]

- C23 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended December 31, 2024 (published January 29, 2025), Raymond James said it has historically had about 1% regretted attrition, and that the biggest movement of people to the RIA channel has gone to its own RIA so the firm kept the assets: “We’ve historically had that 1% type of regretted attrition, but we don’t see anything big on the leaving side. The biggest movement of people that have gone to RIA, fortunately, have gone to our RIA, so we’ve kept the assets.” [RJF-TR-2024Q4#49]
- C24 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended September 30, 2025 (published October 22, 2025), Raymond James said it expects to lose a platform of about $2.7 billion of assets through bank M&A: “From time to time with bank M&A, next quarter, for example, there’ll be a platform that we’ll lose just through bank M&A. I think it’s a $2.7 billion of assets.” [RJF-TR-2025Q3#46]
